We now describe some examples of theories naming an algebraically embedded predicate from the existing literature. We first prove the following lemma, which can be used to characterize algebraically embedded definable sets in simple theories.

Lemma 4.14
.

Suppose
T
is simple. Fix
A,B,C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and assume
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
. Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{ABC}}B
.

(ii)

ABC\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{AC},\overline{BC}}\overline{ABC}
.

(iii)

\overline{ABC}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{AC},\overline{BC})
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume
C\subseteq A\cap B
(replacing
A
and
B
with
AC
and
BC
has no effect on the statement). We will make tacit use of the fact that since
T^{\mathrm{eq}}
is simple,
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is symmetric and so base monotonicity holds on the left and transitivity holds on the right. To ease notation, we write
\mathrm{acl}
and
\mathrm{dcl}
for
\mathrm{acl}_{T}^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
\mathrm{dcl}_{T}^{\mathrm{eq}}
.

(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
. Assume
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{AB}}B
. By existence and extension there is
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{U}
such that
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}\equiv^{T}_{\overline{AB}}AB
and
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{AB}}AB
.

Claim.

A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CA^{\prime}}BB^{\prime}
.

Proof.
Note first that
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A}}\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
by Lemma
4.10
. So, as
C\subseteq A
,
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}C\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}

(\ast)
by (left) base monotonicity. Since
\overline{AB}=\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
(as sets), we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}}B
. Using transitivity with
(\ast)
yields
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}B\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}

(\ast\ast)
. Next, since
AB\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}}A^{\prime}B^{\prime}
, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{B\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}}A^{\prime}BB^{\prime}
by base monotonicity. Using transitivity with
(\ast\ast)
then yields
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}A^{\prime}BB^{\prime}
. So
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{C\overline{A}A^{\prime}}BB^{\prime}
by base monotonicity. Since
\overline{A}=\overline{A^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}(A^{\prime})
, we have
\mathrm{acl}(C\overline{A}A^{\prime})=\mathrm{acl}(CA^{\prime})
. So
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CA^{\prime}}BB^{\prime}
by Remark
4.2
(i)
.

\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Now, as
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{AB}}A^{\prime}
and
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A}}\overline{AB}
, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A}}A^{\prime}
by transitivity, and hence
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}CA^{\prime}\ (*)
by base monotonocity. We also have
B\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{B}}\overline{AB}
, and hence
B\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A},\overline{B}}\overline{AB}\ (**)
by base monotonicity. From the claim, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CA^{\prime}}BB^{\prime}
, hence by
(*)
we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}BA^{\prime}B^{\prime}
. As
\overline{AB}=\overline{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
we get
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{A}}B\overline{AB}
, hence
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{B\overline{A}}\overline{AB}
. Using
(**)
we obtain
AB\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{A},\overline{B}}\overline{AB}
.

(ii)\Rightarrow(iii)
. This follows from Remark
4.2
(ii)
and the fact that

\overline{AB}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}(AB)
.

(iii)\Rightarrow(i)
. First recall that
\overline{A}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}(A)
and
\overline{B}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}(B)
. Thus we have
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{C\overline{A},\overline{B}}B
by base monotonicity and the assumption that
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
. Therefore
(i)
follows from
(iii)
(using Remark
4.2
(i)
and base monotonicity).
∎

Recall that a
belle paire
of models of a theory
T_{0}
is a pair
(M,N)
where
M,N\models T_{0}
,
N\preceq M
,
N
is
|T|^{+}
-saturated, and for any finite
A\subseteq M
, any type over
NA
is realized in
M
. If
T_{0}
is stable with nfcp then the theory of
belle paires
of
T_{0}
is stable (and nfcp). For further background, see
[
Poi83
,
BYPV03
]
.

Corollary 4.15
.

Suppose
T_{0}
is a stable theory with nfcp. Let
T
be the theory of belle paires of
T_{0}
, and let
Q
name the elementary submodel. Then
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
.

Proof.

Given a set
D\subseteq\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{eq}}
, let
D^{c}\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
be the canonical base of
D
over
Q
in the sense of
T_{0}
. In particular, we have
D^{c}\subseteq\overline{D}
and for all
B\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
D\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{D^{c}}B
, where
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
denotes forking independence in
T_{0}^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then by
[
BYPV03
, Proposition 7.3]
,
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
if and only if
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CQ}B
and
(AC)^{c}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C^{c}}(BC)^{c}
. Now suppose
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
. Then, as
\overline{ABC}\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{ABC}Q}B
.
Note also that
(AC\overline{ABC})^{c}
and
(BC\overline{ABC})^{c}
are contained in
\overline{ABC}
, and that
\overline{ABC}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{(C\overline{ABC})^{c}}\overline{ABC}
.
Altogether, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{ABC}}B
. The result now follows from Lemma
4.14
.
∎

Suppose
T_{0}
is a superstable theory with
U
-rank
1
. Then an
H
-structure
of
T_{0}
is a structure
(M,H)
where
M
is a model of
T
and
H
is a new unary predicate naming an algebraically independent subset of
M
that satisfies certain density and extension properties for types. See
[
BV16
]
for details (the construction works more generally for any geometric theory, but we focus on the
U
-rank
1
case). In this case, there is a well-defined theory of
H
-structures of
T_{0}
. Moreover,
T
is superstable (see
[
BV16
, Theorem 5.5, Proposition 5.24]
) and so
H
is stably embedded in
T
.

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
T_{0}
be a superstable theory of SU-rank 1, and let
T
be the theory of
H
-structures of
T_{0}
. Then
H
is algebraically embedded in
T
.

Proof.

We work in a monster model
\mathcal{U}
of
T
, and let
H=H(\mathcal{U})
. Let
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
denote forking independence in
T_{0}
.
By
[
BCV17
, Proposition 4.5]
, for every
C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and finite tuple
a\in\mathcal{U}
, there is a unique minimal finite subset
H_{0}\subseteq H
such that
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CH_{0}}H
. This subset
H_{0}
is denoted
\textsc{HB}(a/C)
.
Given sets
A,C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
, define
\textsc{HB}(A/C)
to be the union of
\textsc{HB}(a/C)
for all finite tuples
a
from
A
. Then
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\textsc{HB}(A/C)}H
by
4
and
3
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. By
[
BCV17
, Theorem 5.3]
, for any
A,B,C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
with
B=\mathrm{acl}_{T}(B)
and
C=\mathrm{acl}_{T}(C)
, we have
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
if and only if
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CH}B
and
\textsc{HB}(A/C)=\textsc{HB}(A/BC)
. Let
\textsc{HB}(A)
denote
\textsc{HB}(A/\emptyset)
.

Claim.
If
A\subseteq\mathcal{U}
then
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(C\textsc{HB}(A))=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(C\overline{A})
.

Proof.
It suffices to assume
C=\emptyset
. So fix
A\subseteq\mathcal{U}
. Then
\textsc{HB}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{T}(A)
by
[
BCV17
, Corollary 4.13]
. So using Lemma
4.9
, we have
\textsc{HB}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(A)\cap H^{\mathrm{eq}}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\overline{A})
, hence
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\textsc{HB}(A))\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{A})
. For the other containment, first note that
\textsc{HB}(A/\textsc{HB}(A))=\emptyset=\textsc{HB}(A/H)
. Therefore
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\textsc{HB}(A)}H
by the above characterization of
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. Using Remark
4.2
(ii)
, we then have
\overline{A}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{T}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\cap\ \mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(H)\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{T}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\textsc{HB}(A))
. So
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{A})\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\textsc{HB}(A))
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Now, for any
A,B,C,D\subseteq\mathcal{U}
, if
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\textsc{HB}(D)}B
then
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\overline{D}}B
by the claim and Remark
4.2
(i)
. It then follows from Lemma
4.14
that in order to prove
H
is algebraically embedded in
T
, it is enough to prove that for any
A,B,C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
, if
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
then
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C\textsc{HB}(ABC)}B
.

Fix
A,B,C\subseteq\mathcal{U}
with
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{C}B
. Set
D=\textsc{HB}(ABC)
. We want to show
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CD}B
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
B=\mathrm{acl}_{T}(B)
and
C=\mathrm{acl}_{T}(C)
. So
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CH}B
, which implies
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CDH}B
since
D\subseteq H
. Further, we have
ABC\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{D}H
so
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CD}H
and
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 0$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{BCD}H
. It follows that

\textsc{HB}(A/CD)=\emptyset=\textsc{HB}(A/BCD).

Thus we conclude that
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{CD}B
, as desired.
∎

In Section
7.2
, we will provide more examples of algebraically embedded definable sets in stable expansions of
(\mathbb{Z},+)
. In fact, we currently do not know an example of a simple theory
T
and definable set
Q
that is not algebraically embedded.
On the other hand, if
T
is NSOP
1
then it is more natural to consider a stronger version of algebraic embeddedness in which
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is replaced by Kim-independence. In this case, we can give a counterexample.

Example 4.17
.

We describe an NSOP
1
theory with a stably embedded
\emptyset
-definable set that is not algebraically embedded with respect to Kim-independence (as explained above). Let
p
be a fixed positive prime and let ACFG be the theory of generic algebraically closed fields of characteristic
p
with a distinguished additive subgroup. Then ACFG is NSOP
1
and not simple (see
[
d’E21
]
). Let
(K,G)
be a monster model of ACFG and let
\pi
be the canonical projection from the additive group of
K
to the quotient group
V=K/G
. Consider the two sorted structure
(K,V,\pi:K\rightarrow V)
, whose theory is denoted
T
. From
[
d’E21
, Theorem 3.15]
,
T
has weak elimination of imaginaries. One can also deduce the following results from
[
d’E21
, Section 3]
:

(1)

V
is stably embedded in
(K,V,\pi)
and
V_{\textnormal{ind}}
is a pure
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector space of infinite dimension; in particular it has weak elimination of imaginaries.

(2)

For any
A\subseteq K
,
\mathrm{acl}_{T}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)=\mathrm{acl}_{\mathrm{ACF}}(A)\cup\langle{\pi(\mathrm{acl}_{\mathrm{ACF}}(A))}\rangle
. In particular,
\overline{A}\subseteq\pi(\mathrm{acl}_{\mathrm{ACF}}(A))
.

Fix
(M,\pi(M))\prec(K,V)
and let
A,B
be algebraically closed subsets of
K
containing
M
. Then
A
and
B
are Kim-independent over
(M,\pi(M))
if and only if
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{ACF}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}\ B
and
\pi(A)\cap\pi(B)=\pi(M)
. It is left to the reader to check that there exists
A
and
B
Kim-independent over
(M,\pi(M))
such that

\pi(\mathrm{acl}_{\mathrm{ACF}}(AB))\nsubseteq\pi(A)\oplus_{\pi(M)}\pi(B)=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\pi(A),\pi(B))\cap V.

We conclude that
V
is not algebraically embedded (for Kim-independence) in
T
.

4.3.
Adding
\mathcal{Q}

We now fix an arbitrary structure
\mathcal{Q}
expanding
Q_{\textnormal{ind}}
, and let
\mathcal{U}
be a monster model of
T[\mathcal{Q}]
. As before, we identify
Q
with
Q(\mathcal{U})
and
\mathcal{Q}
with its interpretation in
\mathcal{U}
(so
\mathcal{Q}
is a monster model of
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
). We write
\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}
for
\equiv^{\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{eq}})}
.

Given a tuple
a
in
\mathcal{U}
, we will uniformly choose an enumeration for
\overline{a}
. Precisely, we proceed as follows: given an ordinal
\kappa
, we fix an enumeration of all (potentially partial)
\emptyset
-definable functions, in the variables
(x_{i})_{i<\kappa}
, into
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. For any tuple
a
of
\mathcal{U}
of length
\kappa
we apply these definable functions in order to
a
(substituting
a_{i}
for
x_{i}
) whenever it makes sense. We denote
\overline{a}
the resulting tuple in
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
.

Remark 4.18
.

Let
a,a^{\prime}
be two small tuples from
\mathcal{U}
.
We note that if
a\equiv a^{\prime}
then
a\overline{a}\equiv a^{\prime}\overline{a^{\prime}}
and that if
a\equiv_{\overline{a}}a^{\prime}
then
\overline{a}=\overline{a^{\prime}}
(as tuples).

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
c
be a tuple from
\mathcal{U}
.

(a)

Given
d,e\in Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
d\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}e
if and only if
d\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{c}}e
.

(b)

Given
a\in\mathcal{U}
and
e\in Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
, if
e\equiv_{\overline{c}}^{\mathcal{Q}}\overline{a}
then there is some
b\in\mathcal{U}
such that
b\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}a
and
\overline{b}=e
.

(c)

Given
a,b\in\mathcal{U}
, we have
a\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}b
if and only if
a\equiv^{T}_{c}b
and
\overline{ac}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{c}}\overline{bc}
.

Proof.

Part
(a)
. The forward direction is clear. Conversely, let
\sigma
be a
\mathcal{Q}
-automorphism over
\overline{c}
sending
d
to
e
. As in the proof of Proposition
2.8
(a)
, we may assume
\mathcal{L}
includes constants for
c
and thus, by stable embeddedness, we may extend
\sigma
to an
\mathcal{L}[\mathcal{Q}]
-automorphism of
\mathcal{U}
over
c
.

Part
(b)
. Assume
e\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{c}}\overline{a}
. Then
e\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}\overline{a}
by part
(a)
. So there is some
b\in\mathcal{U}
such that
be\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}a\overline{a}
. Since
\overline{a}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(a)
, and we have chosen a canonical enumeration, it follows that
\overline{b}=e
.

Part
(c)
. The forward direction is clear. For the converse, assume
a\equiv^{T}_{c}b
and
\overline{ac}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{c}}\overline{bc}
. Note that
a\equiv^{T}_{c}b
implies
a\overline{ac}\equiv^{T}_{c}b\overline{bc}
(since have we chosen canonical enumerations of
\overline{ac}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(ac)
and
\overline{bc}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{eq}_{T}(bc)
). By Lemma
2.5
(c)
, we have
\mathrm{tp}^{T}(a/c\overline{ac})\vdash\mathrm{tp}^{T}(a/cQ)
. So we can apply Proposition
2.8
(a)
, where
c
is
\overline{ac}
,
B
is
c
and
E
is
\overline{c}
, to conclude
a\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}b
.
∎

Lemma 4.20
.

Suppose
M\prec\mathcal{U}
and
B\subseteq\mathcal{U}
contains
M
. Fix a tuple
a\in Q
and assume
\mathrm{tp}^{Q_{\textnormal{ind}}}(a/\overline{B})
does not divide over
\overline{M}
in
\operatorname{Th}(Q_{\textnormal{ind}})
. Then
\mathrm{tp}^{T}(a/B)
does not divide over
M
in
T
.

Proof.

Let
b
enumerate
B
and choose an
M
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
with
b_{0}=b
. Then
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i<\omega}
is
M
-indiscernible in
T
, and hence
\overline{M}
-indiscernible in
\operatorname{Th}(Q_{\textnormal{ind}})
. By assumption, there is some
e\in Q(\mathcal{U})
such that
e\overline{b}_{i}\equiv^{Q_{\textnormal{ind}}}_{\overline{M}}a\overline{b}
for all
i<\omega
. Given
i<\omega
, since
b\equiv^{T}_{M}b_{i}
, we find some
e_{i}\in Q(\mathcal{U})
with
ab\equiv^{T}_{M}e_{i}b_{i}
. Then
e\overline{b_{i}}\equiv^{Q_{\textnormal{ind}}}_{\overline{M}}a\overline{b}\equiv^{Q_{\textnormal{ind}}}_{\overline{M}}e_{i}\overline{b_{i}}
and, by Lemma
4.19
(a)
,
e\equiv^{T}_{b_{i}}e_{i}
. So
eb_{i}\equiv^{T}_{M}e_{i}b_{i}\equiv^{T}_{M}ab
, for all
i<\omega
.
∎

4.4.
Strong types over
Q
and the structure
H

The strategy to prove preservation of simplicity and NSOP
1
is to identify forking and apply Kim-Pillay type results
[
KP97
]
, see Theorem
4.35
and Corollary
4.36
. One of the more technical steps is to prove that the potential forking-independence verifies the independence theorem, or equivalently 3-amalgamation. Our approach to proving this fact is to first amalgamate in
Q
and then to amalgamate over
Q
. This however requires understanding strong types over
Q
. We solve that issue by replacing
Q
by a collection of definable sets
H=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{D}}X
, such that strong types over
Q(\mathcal{U})
coincide with types over
H(\mathcal{U})
; provided
T
eliminates hyperimaginaries.

Let
T
be a complete
\mathcal{L}
-theory, and fix an
\emptyset
-definable stably embedded set
Q
. Let
\mathcal{U}
be a sufficiently saturated monster model of
T
. Fix a small model
M\prec\mathcal{U}
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be the collection of
M
-definable subsets of
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{eq}}
that admit an
M
-definable finite-to-one map to
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
.

Proposition 4.21
.

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U})=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{D}}X(\mathcal{U})=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
.

Proof.

The left-to-right containment is clear. Conversely, suppose
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
. Let
\varphi(x,y)
be an
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{M}
-formula such that, for some tuple
b
from
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
\varphi(x,b)
isolates
\mathrm{tp}^{T^{\mathrm{eq}}}(e/MQ)
. By modifying
\varphi(x,y)
if necessary, we may assume that
\varphi(x,b^{\prime})
is algebraic for any
b^{\prime}
, and that for any
b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}
,
\varphi(x,b^{\prime})
and
\varphi(x,b^{\prime\prime})
are either equal or disjoint. Now let
X
be defined by
\exists y\varphi(x,y)
, and define
f\colon X\to Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
such that, given
a\in X(\mathcal{U})
,
f(a)
is the canonical parameter of
\varphi(x,b^{\prime})
for some/any
b^{\prime}
from
Q
such that
\varphi(a,b^{\prime})
holds.
∎

We now define
H
. The sorts of
H
are given by
\mathcal{D}
. For each
X\in\mathcal{D}
we interpret the universe
X(H)
as a new copy of
X(\mathcal{U})
. Let
\iota_{X}\colon X(\mathcal{U})\to X(H)
be a bijection witnessing the copy.
We put the
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{M}
-induced structure on (the universe of)
H
as follows. For any
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in\mathcal{D}
and any
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{M}
-definable
Y\subseteq X_{1}\times\ldots\times X_{n}
, we add a relation
R
on
X_{1}(H)\times\ldots\times X_{n}(H)
such that
(\iota_{X_{1}}(a_{1}),\ldots,\iota_{X_{n}}(a_{n}))\in R
if and only if
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in Y
.
In particular,
\iota_{X}
is an isomorphism between
X(\mathcal{U})
and
X(H)
(as models of the
T_{M}
-induced structure on
X
).

We also define the structure
\mathcal{U}_{H}=(\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{eq}},H,(\iota_{X})_{X\in\mathcal{D}})
in which we add
H
to
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{eq}}
in its own set of sorts. Note that any automorphism of
\mathcal{U}
over
M
uniquely extends to
\mathcal{U}_{H}
.

Remark 4.22
.

(1)

The universe of
H
is a union of sorts
\mathcal{U}_{M}^{\mathrm{eq}}
(using the right definition), and
H
is precisely the induced structure on this universe. Note also that the
\iota_{X}
maps are already included in
\mathcal{U}_{M}^{\mathrm{eq}}
. So altogether
\mathcal{U}_{H}
is a reduct of
\mathcal{U}_{M}^{\mathrm{eq}}
. We chose to keep them separate in the hope of a clearer setup.

(2)

By construction, the
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{U}_{H})
-induced structure on the universe of
H
is
H
.

(3)

Let
H(M)
denote the substructure of
\mathcal{H}
consisting of
X(M)
for all sorts
X
in
H
. Then
H(M)\prec\mathcal{H}
. In fact,
H(M)
is the definable closure of
\emptyset
in
H
and thus a prime model. Note that if
m
enumerates
M^{\mathrm{eq}}
then
\hat{m}
(as defined in the next paragraph) enumerates
H(M)
.

Let us now define a map from tuples in
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
to tuples in
H
.
First consider a singleton
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{e}=\{X\in\mathcal{D}:e\in X(\mathcal{U})\}
. We define
\hat{e}=(\iota_{X}(e))_{X\in\mathcal{D}_{e}}\in\prod_{X\in\mathcal{D}_{e}}X(H)
; where we implicitly choose a total order on the elements of
\mathcal{D}
. Then, given a tuple
(e_{i})_{i\in I}
from
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
, let
\hat{e}=(\hat{e}_{i})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{X\in\mathcal{D}_{e_{i}}}X(H)
. Note that if
a,b
are tuples from
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
then
\widehat{ab}=\hat{a}\hat{b}
.

Let us prove some basic facts about the structure
H
:

Lemma 4.23
.

Suppose
e
is a tuple from
H
and
e\equiv^{H}\hat{b}
for some tuple
b\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
. Then there is some tuple
a\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
such that
e=\hat{a}
.

Proof.

It follows from Remark
4.22
(2)
, that
e\equiv^{\mathcal{U}_{H}}\hat{b}
. So we find
a\in\mathcal{U}
such that
ea\equiv^{\mathcal{U}_{H}}\hat{b}b
. But
\hat{b}\in\mathrm{dcl}_{\mathcal{U}_{H}}(b)
and therefore
e=\hat{a}
.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

H
eliminates imaginaries.

Proof.

Since sorts of
H
are closed under products, it suffices, given
X\in\mathcal{D}
, some
\emptyset
-definable equivalence relation
E
on
X
and some
a\in X(H)
, to find some tuple
b\in H
interdefinable with
a_{E}
. Let
c=\iota_{X}^{\text{-}1}(a)\in X(\mathcal{U})
. Note that
E
induces an
M
-definable equivalence relation on
X(\mathcal{U})
(via
\iota_{X}
), also denoted
E
. Then
c_{E}\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(Mc)\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
. Then
a_{E}
and
b=\widehat{c_{E}}
are interdefinable in
\mathcal{U}_{H}^{\mathrm{eq}}
and therefore, by Remark
4.22
(2)
, in
H^{\mathrm{eq}}
.
∎

From now on, we will assume that any subset of
\mathcal{U}
is an extension basis for nonforking in
T
, denoted
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. This holds in particular if
T
is simple. We also assume that
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
.

We say that (the collection of sorts of)
H
is
stably embedded
if any
\mathcal{U}_{H}
-definable subset
Y\subseteq X(M)^{n}
, for some
X\in\mathcal{D}
, is
H
-definable. Recall that sorts of
H
are closed under products.

Lemma 4.25
.

The collection of the sorts of
H
is stably embedded in
\mathcal{U}_{H}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\iota_{X}^{-1}(Y)\subseteq X(U)
is definable over some tuple
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
. Indeed
Y
is then definable over
\hat{e}\subseteq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U})
. So for the rest of the proof we work only in
T^{\mathrm{eq}}
, and we identify
X
with
X(\mathcal{U})
.

Let
\varphi(x,y)
be an
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{eq}}
-formula such that
\varphi(x,a)
defines
Y
for some real tuple
a
from
\mathcal{U}
. Let
e
be the canonical parameter for
\varphi(x,a)
. To show that
Y
is definable over
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
, it suffices to show that
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
.

Choose some
N\preceq\mathcal{U}
containing
M
and
a
. Since
X\in\mathcal{D}
, we have an
M
-definable finite-to-one function
f\colon X\to Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Let
S=\{\mathrm{tp}(c/Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N)):c\in f(X)\}
. Since
MQ(N)
is an extension basis, we may fix a Morley sequence
(a_{i}e_{i})_{i<\kappa}
in
\mathrm{tp}^{T}(ae/MQ(N))
with
a_{0}e_{0}=ae
and
\kappa>2^{|S|}+\aleph_{0}
. Let
Y_{i}
be the set defined by
\varphi(x,a_{i})
, which has canonical parameter
e_{i}
.

Claim.

Y_{i}=Y_{j}
for some
i<j<\kappa
.

Note that if the claim holds, then
e_{i}=e
for all
i<\kappa
, and so
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ(N))
. So it suffices to prove the claim.

Proof of the claim.
For
i<\kappa
, let
A_{i}=a_{i}MQ(N)
. Then
Q(N)\subseteq A_{0}\subseteq N
and so
\overline{A_{0}}=Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N)
. So
\overline{A_{i}}=Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N)
for all
i<\kappa
by Remark
4.8
. For any
i<j<\kappa
, we have
A_{i}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{MQ(N)}A_{j}
and so, since
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
,

\overline{A_{i}A_{j}}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{A_{i}},\overline{A_{j}})\cap Q^{\mathrm{eq}}=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N))\cap Q^{\mathrm{eq}}=Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N).

Note that each
Y_{i}
is contained in
X
since
a_{i}\equiv_{M}a
.
Given
c\in f(X)
and
i<\kappa
, let
Y_{i,c}=\{x\in Y_{i}:f(x)=c\}
. Given
i<j
, let
Z_{i,j}=\{c\in f(X):Y_{i,c}=Y_{j,c}\}
. Then each
Z_{i,j}
is an
a_{i}a_{j}M
-definable subset of
f(X)
. Since
Q
is stably embedded, it follows from Remark
2.6
(a)
that each
Z_{i,j}
is definable over
\overline{a_{i}a_{j}M}\subseteq\overline{A_{i}A_{j}}\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N)
.

Now fix
p\in S
. Define a map
g_{p}
on
\kappa
such that
g_{p}(i)=\{x\in Y_{i}:f(x)\models p\}
. We claim that
g_{p}
has finite image. Indeed, suppose
I\subseteq\kappa
is infinite and fix some
c\models p
. Since each
Y_{i,c}
is a subset of
f^{\text{-}1}(c)
, which is finite, there are distinct
i,j\in I
such that
Y_{i,c}=Y_{j,c}
, i.e.,
c\in Z_{i,j}
. Since
Z_{i,j}
is definable over
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}(N)
, we therefore have
Y_{i,d}=Y_{j,d}
for all
d\models p
, and so
g_{p}(i)=g_{p}(j)
.

Finally, define the map
g
on
\kappa
such that
g(i)=(g_{p}(i))_{p\in S}
. By the above, the image of
g
has size at most
2^{|S|}+\aleph_{0}
. So there are
i<j<\kappa
such that
g(i)=g(j)
, i.e.,
Y_{i}=Y_{j}
.
∎

We now fix an expansion
\mathcal{Q}
of
Q_{\textnormal{ind}}
and assume
\mathcal{U}\models T[\mathcal{Q}]
is a monster model. Let
H
be as above. We use
\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}
to denote the structure
((\mathcal{U}|_{T})^{\mathrm{eq}},H,(\iota_{X})_{X\in\mathcal{D}})
and let
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{H}}
be the expansion of
\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}
obtained by expanding
Q
with
\mathcal{Q}
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be the structure on (the universe of)
H
induced by
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{H}})
. Let
\iota_{H}
denote the surjective function from (the universe of)
H
to
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
(inside
\mathcal{U}
) given by
\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{D}}\iota^{\text{-}1}_{X}
. Given a set
A\subseteq H
, we let
\widehat{A}
denote the set
\widehat{\iota_{H}(A)}
, and given a tuple
a
from
H
, we let
\hat{a}
denote the tuple
\widehat{\iota_{H}(a)}
.

We now indicate how some of the results stated in Section
2
on a single stably embedded set, namely
Q
, generalise to
H
which is a stably embedded collection of
\emptyset
-definable sets. For any tuple
a
in
\mathcal{U}
, let
\overline{a}^{H}
(uniformly) enumerate
\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathcal{U}_{H}}(a)\cap H
.

Lemma 4.26
.

For any tuple
a
in
\mathcal{U}
,
\mathrm{tp}(a/\overline{a}^{H})\vdash\mathrm{tp}(a/H)
and any automorphism of
H
over
\overline{a}^{H}
extends to an automorphism of
\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}
over
a
. In particular, any automorphism of
\mathcal{H}
over
\overline{a}^{H}
extends to an automorphism of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{H}}
over
a
.

Proof.

For the first statement, we proceed as in Lemma
2.5
(c)
, using the fact that
H
eliminates imaginaries (Lemma
4.24
) to find canonical parameters in
H
directly.

Since
H
is stably embedded (even with
a
named) and we just showed that the
a
-induced structure on
H
in
\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}
is given by naming
\overline{a}^{H}
, the second statement follows from the proof of
[
CH99
, Lemma 1, Appendix]
, which extends word for word to the context of a stably embedded collection of
\emptyset
-definable sets.
∎

Lemma 4.27
.

Suppose
a
is a tuple from
\mathcal{U}
containing
M
, and let
c
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{a})
. Then
\overline{a}^{H}\subseteq\hat{c}
.

Proof.

Fix
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathcal{U}_{H}}(a)\cap H
. Then there is some
X\in\mathcal{D}
and some
b\in X(\mathcal{U})
such that
e=\iota_{X}(b)
. Since
\iota_{X}
is a bijection,
b\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathcal{U}_{H}}(e)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathcal{U}_{H}}(a)\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{eq}}=\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(a)
(recall that
a
contains
M
). By assumption, there is a
M
-definable finite-to-one map
f\colon X(\mathcal{U})\to Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then
f(b)\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(b)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(a)
, and so
f(b)\in\overline{a}
. Therefore
b\in c
since
f
is finite-to-one and
M
-definable. So
e=\iota_{X}(b)\in\hat{c}
.
∎

Lemma 4.28
.

Suppose
a,b,c
are tuples in
H
. Then
a\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{c}b
if and only if
\hat{a}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{c}}\hat{b}
, if and only if
\iota_{H}(a)\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{\iota_{H}(c)}\iota_{H}(b)
.

Proof.

Since
a\subseteq\hat{a}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{H}(a)
, the first equivalence follows. As for the second, it follows from Lemma
4.26
and the fact that any automorphism of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{H}}
preserves
\iota_{X}
.
∎

Lemma 4.29
.

Suppose
a,b
are tuples from
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
, and
c
is a tuple from
\mathcal{U}
containing
M
. Let
c_{*}
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{c})
, and assume
\hat{a}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{c}_{*}}\hat{b}
. Then
a\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{c}b
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.27
, we have
\overline{c}^{H}\subseteq\hat{c}_{*}
and hence
\hat{a}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\overline{c}^{H}}\hat{b}
. Let
\sigma
be a
\mathcal{H}
-automorphism over
\overline{c}^{H}
sending
\hat{a}
to
\hat{b}
. Then, by Lemma
4.26
,
\sigma
extends to an automorphism of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{H}}
over
c
, which commutes with
\iota_{X}
and hence sends
a
to
b
.
∎

We can also generalise Lemma
4.20
to
H
, following the same argument but using Lemma
4.26
instead of Lemma
4.19
(a)
. A proof of a very similar result (but for Kim-forking) is given in detail in the proof of Claim 3 of Lemma
4.32
.

Lemma 4.30
.

Suppose
N\prec\mathcal{U}
contain
M
and
B\subseteq\mathcal{U}
contains
N
. Fix a tuple
a\in H
and assume
\mathrm{tp}^{H}(a/\overline{B}^{H})
does not divide over
\overline{N}^{H}
in
\operatorname{Th}(H)
. Then
\mathrm{tp}^{\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}}(a/B)
does not divide over
N
in
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H})
.

Given a set
A\subseteq H
, we let
\overline{A}
denote the set
\overline{\iota_{H}(A)}
, and given a tuple
a
from
H
, we let
\overline{a}
denote the tuple
\overline{\iota_{H}(a)}
.

Remark 4.31
.

Note that, for any tuples
a,b,c\in H
, if
a\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{c}b
, then
\overline{a}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{c}}\overline{c}
. This follows from Lemmas
4.28
and
4.19
(a)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
denote the expansion of
Q^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\textnormal{ind}}
by
\mathcal{Q}
(so
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
is an intermediate structure between
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{eq}}
). Note that if
N\prec\mathcal{H}
then
\overline{N}\prec\mathcal{Q}^{*}
and
\iota_{H}(N)
contains
M
.

We now assume that
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
. Let
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
and
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
denote Kim-dividing independence in
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{eq}}
, respectively.

Lemma 4.32
.

Suppose
N\prec\mathcal{H}
and
a,b\in H
are tuples containing
N
. Then

a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}b\iff\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{b}.

Proof.

Given a tuple
e
from
H
, let
\tilde{e}
denote
\hat{\overline{e}}
. Note that
\tilde{e}\in\mathrm{dcl}_{\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}}(e)\cap H=\mathrm{dcl}_{H}(e)
.

Now suppose
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}b
. Fix an
N
-invariant global
\mathcal{H}
-type
q
extending
\mathrm{tp}^{\mathcal{H}}(b/N)
, and a Morley sequence
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
in
q
over
N
with
b_{0}=b
. By
[
KR20
, Lemma 3.18]
, there exists
c\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{b}a
such that
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
is
Nc
-indiscernible in
\mathcal{H}
. Then, by Remark
4.31
,
\overline{c}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{b}}\overline{a}
.

Let
\overline{q}
denote the type (in
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
) whose restriction to any
A\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
is the type of
\overline{b}
for any
b\models q|_{\widehat{A}}
. This is a complete type by Remark
4.31
. Then
\overline{q}
is
\overline{N}
-invariant, since, by Lemmas
4.28
and
4.19
(a)
, for any
e_{1},e_{2}\in Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
, if
e_{1}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{N}}e_{2}
then
\hat{e}_{1}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\hat{e}_{2}
.

Claim 1.

(i)

(\overline{b}_{i})_{i<\omega}
is a Morley sequence in
\overline{q}
over
\overline{N}
.

(ii)

(\overline{b}_{i})_{i<\omega}
is indiscernible over
\overline{N}\overline{c}
.

Proof.

Part
(i)
. Fix
i<\omega
. We have
b_{i}\models q|_{Nb_{<i}}
and hence, since
\tilde{b}_{i}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{H}(b_{i})
,
b_{i}\models q|_{N\tilde{b}_{<i}}
. It follows, by definition, that
\overline{b}_{i}\models\overline{q}|_{N\overline{b}_{<i}}
.

Part
(ii)
. Fix
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{n}<\omega
and
j_{1}<\ldots<j_{n}<\omega
. Then
b_{i_{1}}\ldots b_{i_{n}}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{Nc}b_{j_{1}}\ldots b_{j_{n}}
, and so, by Remark
4.31
,
\overline{b}_{i_{1}}\ldots\overline{b}_{i_{n}}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{N}\overline{c}}\overline{b}_{j_{1}}\ldots\overline{b}_{j_{n}}
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Recall that
\overline{c}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{b}}\overline{a}
. Since
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, we have
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{b}
by Claim 1,
[
KR20
, Lemma 3.18]
, and
[
KR20
, Theorem 3.16]
.

Conversely, suppose
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{b}
.

Claim 2.

\tilde{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}b
.

Proof.

Fix an
N
-invariant global
\mathcal{H}
-type
q
extending
\mathrm{tp}^{\mathcal{H}}(b/N)
, and a Morley sequence
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
in
q
over
N
with
b_{0}=b
. Let
\overline{q}
be as above. By part
(i)
of Claim 1,
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i<\omega}
is a Morley sequence in
\overline{q}
over
\overline{N}
. Since
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{b}
, there is some
e\in Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
such that for all
i<\omega
,
e\overline{b}_{i}\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{N}}\overline{a}\overline{b}
. Given
i<\omega
, by Lemma
4.28
we have
\iota_{H}(b)\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{\iota_{H}(N)}\iota_{H}(b_{i})
and hence
\overline{a}\iota_{H}(b)\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{\iota_{H}(N)}e_{i}\iota_{H}(b_{i})
, for some
e_{i}\in Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then
e\equiv^{\mathcal{Q}}_{\overline{b_{i}}}e_{i}
whence
e\iota_{H}(b_{i})\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{\iota_{H}(N)}e_{i}\iota_{H}(b_{i})\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{\iota_{H}(N)}\overline{a}\iota_{H}(b)
, which implies
\hat{e}b_{i}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\tilde{a}b
, for all
i<\omega
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Claim 3.

a\in\mathrm{acl}_{H}(\tilde{a})
.

Proof.
We may assume
a\in X(H)
for some
X\in\mathcal{D}
. By assumption, there is an
M
-definable finite-to-one map
f\colon X(\mathcal{U})\to Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. So
f(\iota_{X}^{\text{-}1}(a))\in\overline{a}
and hence
\hat{f}(a)\in\tilde{a}
, where
\hat{f}
is the map, definable in
H
sending any
x
to (any component of)
\widehat{f(\iota_{X}^{\text{-}1}(x))}
. Since
\hat{f}
has finite fibers, we do have
a\in\mathrm{acl}_{H}(\tilde{a})
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Now
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}b
follows from Claims 2 and 3 and Remark
4.3
.
∎

Corollary 4.33
.

\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{H})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
is assumed to be
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
. So
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle q$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is symmetric
[
KR20
]
. Therefore
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle h$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is symmetric by Lemma
4.32
, which implies
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{H})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
by
[
KR20
, Proposition 3.22]
(and its proof).
∎

Now we let
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
and
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
denote Kim-independence in (the
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
theories)
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{eq}})
and
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{H})
, respectively.

Corollary 4.34
.

Suppose
a,b
are tuples in
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
containing
M
. Then
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{b}
if and only if
\hat{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{b}
.

Proof.

Note
\iota_{H}(H(M))=M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Moreover,
\iota_{H}(\hat{e})=e
(as sets) for any tuple
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M)
. So in light of Corollary
4.33
, the claim is a special case of Lemma
4.32
.
∎

4.5.
NSOP
1
and simplicity of
T[\mathcal{Q}]

We now prove our main preservation result for NSOP
1
. Unlike the previous results involving stability, NIP, and NTP
2
, we will need to add some extra assumptions. As discussed before, one assumption is algebraic embeddedness for the set
Q
. In addition to this, we will need to assume that the base theory
T
is simple with elimination of hyperimaginaries, rather than just NSOP
1
. This is due to an extensive use of forking calculus in
T
, and also to ensure Lascar strong types coincide with Shelah strong types (see Remark
4.37
for further discussion).

Theorem 4.35
.

Assume
T
is simple with elimination of hyperimaginaries and
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
. Then
T[\mathcal{Q}]
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
if and only if
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
. Moreover, in this case Kim-independence over models in
T[\mathcal{Q}]
is given by

A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B\makebox[36.135pt]{$\Leftrightarrow$}A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}B\makebox[28.90755pt]{and}\overline{AM}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}~\overline{BM},

where
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is forking independence in
T
and
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
is Kim-independence in
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{eq}})
.

Proof.

Clearly, if
T[\mathcal{Q}]
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
then so is
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
. Assume
\operatorname{Th}(\mathcal{Q})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
.
Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}
be as defined in the theorem. Note that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}
is
\mathcal{L}[\mathcal{Q}]
-automorphism invariant.
We will show that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}
satisfies the axioms listed in Fact
4.6
. First note that, since
T
is stable,
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
satisfies all of these axioms, as well as
3
and
11
over models. As usual, we will also use
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
for forking independence in
T^{\mathrm{eq}}
.

It is straightforward to check that
5
,
2
,
7
, and
8
(all over models) transfer directly from
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
and
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
to
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}
. For clarity, we provide the details for
8
.

Transitivity over models.
Suppose
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{N}B
and
N\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B
, with
M\preceq N\prec\mathcal{U}
and
A,B\subseteq\mathcal{U}
. Then
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}B
and
N\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}B
, and so
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}B
by transitivity for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. Also
\overline{A}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{BN}
(hence
\overline{A}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{N}}\overline{BM}
) and
\overline{N}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{BM}
, and so
\overline{A}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{BM}
by transitivity for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. Altogether,
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B
.

Finite character over models.
Fix
A,B\subseteq\mathcal{U}
and
M\prec\mathcal{U}
, and suppose
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B
for all finite
a\subseteq A
. Then
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}B
for all finite
a\subseteq A
, and so
A\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}B
by
4
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. Now fix some finite
c\subseteq\overline{AM}
. Since
\overline{AM}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(AM)
, there is a finite set
a\subseteq A
such that
c\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(aM)
. Since
\overline{AM}\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
, we have
c\subseteq\overline{aM}
. Therefore
c\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{BM}
since
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B
implies
\overline{aM}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{BM}
. By
4
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
, we now have
\overline{AM}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{BM}
. Altogether,
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}B
.

Extension over models.
Assume that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
. We may assume
M\subseteq a\cap b
. Let
d
be arbitrary. First, as
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{b}
, by
6
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
there is
e\subseteq Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
with
e\equiv_{\overline{b}}^{\mathcal{Q}}\overline{a}
such that
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{bd}
. By Lemma
4.19
(b)
, there exists
a^{\prime}
such that
\overline{a^{\prime}}=e
and
a^{\prime}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{b}a
. Therefore
a^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}b
. Since
e=\overline{a^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(a^{\prime})
, we then have
a^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{Me}be
by (left) base monotonicity for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
and Remark
4.2
(i)
. Since
Q
is algebraically embedded and
a^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}b
, we have
\overline{a^{\prime}b}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(be)
, and thus
a^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{Me}b\overline{a^{\prime}b}
.

By
6
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
, there exists
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{T}_{b\overline{a^{\prime}b}}a^{\prime}
such that
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{Me}bd
. In particular, we have
\overline{a^{\prime\prime}}=e=\overline{a^{\prime}}
and
\overline{a^{\prime\prime}b}=\overline{a^{\prime}b}
. Hence, by Lemma
4.19
(c)
,
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{b}a^{\prime}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{b}a
.
Also, since
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{bd}
, we have
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}bd
by Lemmas
4.4
and
4.20
. Together with
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{Me}bd
, we have
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}bd
by
8
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
. Therefore
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}bd
.

Chain local character.
Let
a
be a finite tuple,
\kappa>\lvert{T}\rvert
a regular cardinal and
M=\bigcup_{i<\kappa}M_{i}
a continuous chain of models
(M_{i})_{i<\kappa}
, with
\lvert{M_{i}}\rvert<\kappa
. We show that there exists
j<\kappa
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M_{j}}M
. First, using
9
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
, there is a set
B\subseteq M
such that
\lvert{B}\rvert\leq\lvert{T}\rvert
and
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{B}M
. Using
3
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
, we may assume without loss of generality that
B\preceq M
is a model. By
[
KRS19
, Corollary 3.11]
and existence, the set of
N\prec\overline{M}
such that
\lvert{T}\rvert\leq\lvert{N}\rvert<\kappa
and
\overline{aB}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}\overline{M}
is a club in
[\overline{M}]^{<\kappa}
(the set of subsets of
\overline{M}
of size
<\kappa
).
6
6

6

For basics on clubs, see e.g.
[
KRS19
, Definition 2.10]
.
In particular, the set

\textstyle\mathcal{C}_{0}:=\left\{{N\prec\overline{M}:\overline{B}\subseteq N,~\lvert{N}\rvert<\kappa,~\overline{aB}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{N}~\overline{M}}\right\}

is a club of
[\overline{M}]^{<\kappa}
. As
\kappa
is regular and the chain is continuous, the set
\left\{{M_{i}:i<\kappa}\right\}
is a club set in
[M]^{<\kappa}
. In particular
\mathcal{C}:=\left\{{M_{i}:B\subseteq M_{i},i<\kappa}\right\}
is also a club of
[M]^{<\kappa}
, and so
\overline{\mathcal{C}}:=\left\{{\overline{M_{i}}:M_{i}\in\mathcal{C}}\right\}
is a club of
[\overline{M}]^{<\kappa}
. The two clubs
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
intersect, hence there exists
j<\kappa
such that
\overline{aB}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M_{j}}}\overline{M}
and
B\subseteq M_{j}
. Since
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
and
aB\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{B}M_{j}
, we obtain
\overline{aM_{j}}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M_{j}}}\overline{M}
. By
3
for
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
, we have
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M_{j}}M
, hence
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M_{j}}M
.

The independence theorem over models.

Let
a,b,c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
contain a small
M\models T[\mathcal{Q}]
with
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
,
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}a
,
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
, and
c_{1}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{M}c_{2}
. Fix an enumeration
e_{1}
of
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{c_{1}})
. Since
e_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(Mc_{1})
, and
c_{1}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{M}c_{2}
, we may then choose an enumeration
e_{2}
of
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(M\overline{c_{2}})
such that
c_{1}e_{1}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{M}c_{2}e_{2}
. Let
a_{*}
and
b_{*}
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{a})
and
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{b})
, respectively.

Claim 1.

\hat{e}_{1}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{H(M)}\hat{e}_{2}
,
\hat{a}_{*}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{b}_{*}
,
\hat{e}_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{a}_{*}
, and
\hat{e}_{2}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{b}_{*}
.

Proof.

Since
e_{1}\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{M}e_{2}
, we have
\hat{e}_{1}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{H(M)}\hat{e}_{2}
by Lemma
4.28
.

Next we have
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
, and so
\overline{a}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{b}
. By Lemma
4.9
,
\overline{a_{*}}=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{a})\cap Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
\overline{b_{*}}=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{b})\cap Q^{\mathrm{eq}}
. So
\overline{a_{*}}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{b_{*}}
. Therefore
\hat{a}_{*}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{b}_{*}
by Corollary
4.34
. Using similar arguments, we have that
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}a
implies
\hat{e}_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{a}_{*}
, and
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
implies
\hat{e}_{2}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{b}_{*}
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Recall that
\mathcal{H}
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
by Corollary
4.33
. So by Claim 1, we can apply the independence theorem over models to find some tuple
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
e^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{a}_{*}\hat{b}_{*}
,
e^{\prime}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{a}_{*}}\hat{e}_{1}
, and
e^{\prime}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{b}_{*}}\hat{e}_{2}
.

Recall that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}b
, and so
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}b
. Since
Q
is algebraically embedded in
T
, it follows that
\overline{ab}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(\overline{a}\overline{b})
, and so

\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{ab})\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{a}\overline{b})=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(a_{*}b_{*}).

Let
g
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(M\overline{ab})
. Then, since every (
M
-)definable relation in
T
between elements
c,d\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U})
gives rise to a relation between (any of the components of)
\hat{c}
and
\hat{d}
in
H
,
\hat{g}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{H}(\widehat{a_{*}b_{*}})=\mathrm{acl}_{H}(\hat{a}_{*}\hat{b}_{*})
. Thus

e^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{g}
.

Now, by Lemma
4.23
, choose some tuple
e
from
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(MQ)
such that
\hat{e}=e^{\prime}
. So
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{g}
,
\hat{e}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{a}_{*}}\hat{e}_{1}
, and
\hat{e}\equiv^{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{b}_{*}}\hat{e}_{2}
. By Lemma
4.29
, we have
e\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{a}e_{1}
and
e\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{b}e_{2}
. Choose
c^{\prime}_{1}
and
c^{\prime}_{2}
such that
c^{\prime}_{1}e\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{a}c_{1}e_{1}
and
c^{\prime}_{2}e\equiv^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}_{b}c_{2}e_{2}
.

Claim 2.

c^{\prime}_{1}\equiv^{T}_{e}c^{\prime}_{2}
,
c^{\prime}_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}a
,
c^{\prime}_{2}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b
, and
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b
.

Proof.
First, we have
c^{\prime}_{1}e\equiv^{T}c_{1}e_{1}\equiv^{T}c_{2}e_{2}\equiv^{T}c^{\prime}_{2}e
, and so
c^{\prime}_{1}\equiv^{T}_{e}c^{\prime}_{2}
.

Next, recall that
c_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}a
and
e_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(Mc_{1})
, and so
c_{1}e_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}a
. Since
c^{\prime}_{1}e\equiv^{T}_{a}c_{1}e_{1}
, we have
c^{\prime}_{1}e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}a
by invariance, and so
c^{\prime}_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}a
by base monotonicity. By a similar argument, we get
c^{\prime}_{2}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b
.

It remains to prove
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b
. Let us first observe that it suffices to prove
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
. Indeed, given this we get
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{aM}b
by base monotonicity. Together with
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}b
, we get
ea\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}b
by transitivity. So
a\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b
by base monotonicity.

So let us prove that
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
. Recall that
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{g}
. Since
\operatorname{Th}(H)
is simple and
H(M)\preceq\mathcal{H}
, we get
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{g}
by Lemma
4.4
, where
\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }
denotes forking independence in
H
. By Lemma
4.27
,
\overline{M}^{H}=H(M)
and
\overline{ab}^{H}\subseteq\hat{g}
. So we have
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}^{H}}\overline{ab}^{H}
. Then
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
by Remark
4.22
(3)
and Lemma
4.30
. Since
e\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}}(\hat{e})
, we have
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
, and thus
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
since
\mathcal{U}^{T}_{H}
is bi-interpretable over
M
with its reduct to a model of
T
.
\dashv_{\text{\scriptsize{claim}}}

Let
a^{\prime}
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(ae)
and
b^{\prime}
enumerate
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(be)
. Then the previous claim implies
c^{\prime}_{1}\equiv^{T}_{e}c^{\prime}_{2}
,
c^{\prime}_{1}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}a^{\prime}
,
c^{\prime}_{2}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b^{\prime}
, and
a^{\prime}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}b^{\prime}
. Note that
e=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{T}(e)
since
e\equiv_{a}^{T[\mathcal{Q}]}e_{1}
and
e_{1}
enumerates an
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}
-closed set. Since
T
is simple and eliminates hyperimaginaries, by
[
Kim14
, Proposition 5.1.19]
we can apply the independence theorem over
e
to obtain some
c
such that
c\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}a^{\prime}b^{\prime}
,
c\equiv^{T}_{a^{\prime}}c^{\prime}_{1}
, and
c\equiv^{T}_{b^{\prime}}c^{\prime}_{2}
.

Claim 3.

c\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\displaystyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\textstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptstyle{}}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt\scriptscriptstyle{}}}_{M}ab
.

Proof.

We need to show
c\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
and
\overline{c}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{ab}
. Note first that we have
c\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{e}ab
and
e\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
, and so
c\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{M}ab
by transitivity. Next, recall that
\hat{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{H}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{H(M)}\hat{g}
, and so
\overline{e}\mathop{\ \ \hbox to0.0pt{\hss$|^{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Q}$\hss}}$\hss}\lower 4.30554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\ \ }_{\overline{M}}\overline{g}
